Check each minterm for unique coverage.\n\nMinterm 0: can be covered by:\n- ~x3&~x2&~x1 (covers 0,1)\n- ~x3&~x1&~x0 (covers 0,4)\nMaybe also ~x3&~x2&~x0? 0=0000, 2=0010 missing. ~x2&~x1&~x0? 0=0000, 8=1000 missing. So 0 not essential alone.\n\nMinterm 1: \n- ~x3&~x2&~x1 (0,1)\n- ~x3&~x1&x0 (1,5)\nPossibly ~x3&~x2&x0? 1=0001, 3=0011 missing. So 1 not essential.\n\nMinterm 4:\n- ~x3&~x1&~x0 (0,4)\n- ~x3&x2&~x1 (4,5)\n- ~x3&x2&~x0 (4,6)\n- x2&~x1&~x0 (4,12)\nSo many.\n\nMinterm 5:\n- ~x3&~x1&x0 (1,5)\n- ~x3&x2&~x1 (4,5)\n- maybe ~x3&x2&x0? 5=0101, 7=0111 missing.\n- x2&~x1&x0? 5=0101, 13=1101 missing.\nSo 5 covered by two options.\n\nMinterm 6:\n- ~x3&x2&~x0 (4,6)\n- x2&x1&~x0 (6,14)\nMaybe ~x3&x1&~x0? 6=0110, 2=0010 missing? 2 is 0 0 1 0, 6 is 0 1 1 0 differ x2 -> ~x3&x1&~x0 covers 2,6. 2 is not one. So no.\nSo 6 covered by two.\n\nMinterm 12:\n- x2&~x1&~x0 (4,12)\n- x3&x2&~x0 (12,14)\nSo 12 covered by two.\n\nMinterm 14:\n- x2&x1&~x0 (6,14)\n- x3&x2&~x0 (12,14)\n- x3&x2&x1 (14,15)\nSo 14 covered by three.\n\nMinterm 15:\n- x3&x2&x1 (14,15)\nOnly this one? Check 15=1111. Can combine with 14 (1110) -> x3&x2&x1. With 13 (1101) missing. With 11 (1011) missing. With 7 (0111) missing. So yes, minterm 15 is only covered by x3&x2&x1. So essential: x3&x2&x1 (covers 14,15).\n\nNow after covering 14,15 with x3&x2&x1, remaining ones: 0,1,4,5,6,12.\n\nCheck minterm 12 now: covered by x2&~x1&~x0 (4,12) and x3&x2&~x0 (12,14) but 14 already covered, so x3&x2&~x0 still could cover 12 alone? Actually if we don't use x3&x2&~x0, 12 must be covered by x2&~x1&~x0. Is x2&~x1&~x0 essential? Minterm 12 can only be covered by these two. Since 14 is already covered, we can either use x2&~x1&~x0 (covers 4,12) or x3&x2&~x"

```text
[BEGIN]
module TopModule (
    input  [3:0] x,
    output       f
);
    assign f = (~x[3] & ~x[2] & ~x[1]) |
               (~x[3] & ~x[1] & x[0]) |
               (~x[3] & x[2] & ~x[1]) |
               (x[2] & x[1] & ~x[0]) |
               (x[3] & x[2] & x[1]);
endmodule
[DONE]
```
